    for col in ("edad", "ingreso_anual"):
        d = df.copy()
        d["bin"] = pd.qcut(d[col], n_bins, duplicates="drop")
        g = d.groupby("bin", observed=True)[cfg.TARGET_INCIDENCE].agg(["mean", "size"])
        for level, (rate, n) in g.iterrows():
            rows.append(
                {
                    "variable": col,
                    "bin": str(level),
                    "visitas": int(n),
                    "tasa_compra": round(float(rate), 4),
                }
            )
    return pd.DataFrame(rows)


# --------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Segmentation input matrix for model M0
# --------------------------------------------------------------------------


def segmentation_matrix(df: pd.DataFrame) -> pd.DataFrame:
    """Client-level demographic matrix, ready for standardization and K-Means.

    One row per client, indexed by ``id``. This is the input of model M0; the
    behavioural variables are deliberately excluded so the segments stay
    interpretable as commercial groups rather than as outcome proxies.
    """
    prof = client_profile(df)
    return prof.set_index(cfg.KEY_CLIENT)[cfg.DEMOGRAPHIC_COLS]


def behaviour_by_client_for_validation(df: pd.DataFrame) -> pd.DataFrame:
    """Client-level behaviour, held aside to profile segments after clustering.

    Kept separate from :func:`segmentation_matrix` on purpose: clustering on
    behaviour and then explaining behaviour by cluster would be circular.
    """
    prof = client_profile(df)
    return prof.set_index(cfg.KEY_CLIENT)[
        ["visitas", "compras", "tasa_compra", "gasto_total", "ticket_medio"]
    ]

------------------------------------------------------------------------------
ARCHIVO: src/ferreyros/eda/dynamics.py  (409 lineas)
------------------------------------------------------------------------------

"""EDA -- temporal dynamics of demand.

Fase 1 of ``docs/brief-metodologia.md`` asks for "Dinamica temporal:
estacionalidad semanal/mensual, frecuencia de visita, tendencia de la tasa de
compra". Visit frequency already lives in ``constraints.fatigue_bounds`` and
``constraints.visit_interval_profile``; this module covers seasonality, trend,
and the start-of-panel regime that turned out to explain both.

Two caveats are structural and are declared in every function.

The dataset has no calendar dates. ``dia_visita`` is an index from 1 to 730, so
"week" here is the 7-day cycle of that index and "month" a 30-day block. Both
are **proxies**: a cycle effect is read as *there is a cycle*, never as
*Fridays sell more*.

A deviation that beats its standard error is not yet seasonality. Folding 730
days onto 7 or 12 positions and testing each one invites false positives, so
every seasonality verdict here requires the pattern to **replicate across the
two years** of the panel. That test is what turns the apparent weekly and
monthly cycles of this dataset into what they really are: an artifact of the
first 90 days.

Why it matters beyond the report: the features ``semana`` and ``mes`` built in
Fase 2 are trend indices whose test values lie outside the train range (98% and
88% of test rows). Whether that extrapolation is a risk worth taking depends on
whether there is any temporal signal to extrapolate.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import numpy as np
import pandas as pd

from .. import config as cfg

#: Length of the proxy week and month over ``dia_visita``.
DAYS_PER_WEEK = 7
DAYS_PER_MONTH = 30

#: Days per year of the panel, used to split it in two for the replication test.
DAYS_PER_YEAR = 360

#: First block considered already stable when computing the reference rate.
#: Blocks 1-3 are the warm-up this module measures; see :func:`warmup_profile`.
FIRST_STABLE_BLOCK = 4


def _binomial_se(rate: float, n: pd.Series | float) -> pd.Series | float:
    """Standard error of a proportion, used as the noise floor of a cell."""
    return np.sqrt(rate * (1.0 - rate) / n)


def _cycle_profile(df: pd.DataFrame, pos: pd.Series) -> pd.DataFrame:
    """Rate per cycle position, with the two years side by side.

    The correlation between the two yearly profiles is the replication test:
    a real cycle repeats, an artifact does not.
    """
    d = df.copy()
    d["posicion"] = pos
    d["anio_panel"] = np.where(d[cfg.KEY_TIME] <= DAYS_PER_YEAR, 1, 2)

    rate = float(df[cfg.TARGET_INCIDENCE].mean())
    out = (
        d.groupby("posicion", observed=True)
        .agg(
            visitas=(cfg.KEY_CLIENT, "size"),
            compras=(cfg.TARGET_INCIDENCE, "sum"),
            tasa_compra=(cfg.TARGET_INCIDENCE, "mean"),
        )
        .reset_index()
    )
    por_anio = d.pivot_table(
        index="posicion", columns="anio_panel", values=cfg.TARGET_INCIDENCE, aggfunc="mean"
    ).rename(columns={1: "tasa_anio_1", 2: "tasa_anio_2"})
    out = out.merge(por_anio.reset_index(), on="posicion", how="left")

    out["desvio_vs_global"] = out["tasa_compra"] - rate
    out["error_estandar"] = _binomial_se(rate, out["visitas"])
    out["supera_ruido"] = out["desvio_vs_global"].abs() > 2 * out["error_estandar"]

    corr = float(out["tasa_anio_1"].corr(out["tasa_anio_2"]))
    out.attrs["correlacion_entre_anios"] = round(corr, 3)
    out.attrs["celdas_sobre_ruido"] = int(out["supera_ruido"].sum())
    out.attrs["amplitud"] = round(float(out["tasa_compra"].max() - out["tasa_compra"].min()), 4)
    out.attrs["tasa_global"] = round(rate, 4)
    out.attrs["replica"] = corr > 0.5

    for col in ("tasa_compra", "tasa_anio_1", "tasa_anio_2", "desvio_vs_global", "error_estandar"):
        out[col] = out[col].round(4)
    return out


# --------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Weekly seasonality
# --------------------------------------------------------------------------


def weekly_seasonality(df: pd.DataFrame) -> pd.DataFrame:
    """Purchase rate by position in the 7-day cycle, tested for replication."""
    out = _cycle_profile(df, ((df[cfg.KEY_TIME] - 1) % DAYS_PER_WEEK) + 1)
    out = out.rename(columns={"posicion": "dia_del_ciclo"})

    visitas_cv = float(out["visitas"].std() / out["visitas"].mean())
    out.attrs["cv_visitas"] = round(visitas_cv, 4)
    out.attrs["estacionalidad_semanal"] = bool(
        out.attrs["celdas_sobre_ruido"] > 0 and out.attrs["replica"]
    )
    out.attrs["lectura"] = (
        "{} de 7 posiciones se desvian mas de 2 errores estandar y la amplitud es {:.4f} "
        "sobre una tasa global de {:.4f}. Pero los perfiles de los dos anios del panel "
        "correlacionan {:+.3f}: el patron {}. El trafico por posicion varia un {:.1%}, "
        "asi que tampoco es un efecto de exposicion."
    ).format(
        out.attrs["celdas_sobre_ruido"],
        out.attrs["amplitud"],
        out.attrs["tasa_global"],
        out.attrs["correlacion_entre_anios"],
        "se repite y es estacionalidad" if out.attrs["replica"] else "no se repite, asi que no es estacionalidad",
        visitas_cv,
    )
    return out


# --------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Monthly seasonality
# --------------------------------------------------------------------------


def monthly_seasonality(df: pd.DataFrame) -> pd.DataFrame:
    """Purchase rate by position in the 12-block cycle, tested for replication."""
    bloque = (df[cfg.KEY_TIME] - 1) // DAYS_PER_MONTH
    out = _cycle_profile(df, (bloque % 12) + 1)
    out = out.rename(columns={"posicion": "mes_del_ciclo"})

    pico = int(out.loc[out["tasa_compra"].idxmax(), "mes_del_ciclo"])
    valle = int(out.loc[out["tasa_compra"].idxmin(), "mes_del_ciclo"])
    out.attrs["pico"] = pico
    out.attrs["valle"] = valle
    out.attrs["estacionalidad_mensual"] = bool(
        out.attrs["celdas_sobre_ruido"] > 0 and out.attrs["replica"]
    )
    out.attrs["lectura"] = (
        "{} de 12 posiciones superan el ruido, con pico en la {} ({:.4f}) y valle en la "
        "{} ({:.4f}) -- amplitud {:.4f}, tres veces la del ciclo semanal. La correlacion "
        "entre los perfiles de los dos anios es {:+.3f}: {}. El valle de la posicion 1 es "
        "el arranque del panel (ver warmup_profile), no un mes flojo."
    ).format(
        out.attrs["celdas_sobre_ruido"],
        pico,
        float(out["tasa_compra"].max()),
        valle,
        float(out["tasa_compra"].min()),
        out.attrs["amplitud"],
        out.attrs["correlacion_entre_anios"],
        "hay estacionalidad mensual"
        if out.attrs["replica"]
        else "el patron no se repite, asi que no es estacionalidad",
    )
    return out


# --------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Start-of-panel regime
# --------------------------------------------------------------------------


def warmup_profile(df: pd.DataFrame) -> pd.DataFrame:
    """Purchase rate per 30-day block against the share of visits with no history.

    The reference rate is computed on **train only** (blocks from
    ``FIRST_STABLE_BLOCK`` up to the frozen split boundary), so deciding where
    the warm-up ends never looks at test.
    """
    d = df.copy()
    d["bloque"] = ((d[cfg.KEY_TIME] - 1) // DAYS_PER_MONTH) + 1
    d["sin_historial"] = (d["ultima_marca_comprada"] == 0) & (d["ultima_cantidad_comprada"] == 0)

    out = (
        d.groupby("bloque", observed=True)
        .agg(
            dia_inicial=(cfg.KEY_TIME, "min"),
            dia_final=(cfg.KEY_TIME, "max"),
            visitas=(cfg.KEY_CLIENT, "size"),
            tasa_compra=(cfg.TARGET_INCIDENCE, "mean"),
            pct_sin_historial=("sin_historial", "mean"),
        )
        .reset_index()
    )

    train = out[(out["bloque"] >= FIRST_STABLE_BLOCK) & (out["dia_final"] <= cfg.SPLIT_DAY)]
    referencia = float(train["tasa_compra"].mean())
    out["umbral"] = referencia - 2 * _binomial_se(referencia, out["visitas"])
    out["en_warmup"] = False

    for i in out.index:
        if out.loc[i, "tasa_compra"] < out.loc[i, "umbral"]:
            out.loc[i, "en_warmup"] = True
        else:
            break

    n_warmup = int(out["en_warmup"].sum())
    dia_fin = int(out.loc[out["en_warmup"], "dia_final"].max()) if n_warmup else 0
    tasa_warmup = (
        float(
            df.loc[df[cfg.KEY_TIME] <= dia_fin, cfg.TARGET_INCIDENCE].mean()
        )
        if n_warmup
        else float("nan")
    )
    tasa_estable = float(df.loc[df[cfg.KEY_TIME] > dia_fin, cfg.TARGET_INCIDENCE].mean())

    for col in ("tasa_compra", "pct_sin_historial", "umbral"):
        out[col] = out[col].round(4)

    out.attrs["referencia_train"] = round(referencia, 4)
    out.attrs["bloques_warmup"] = n_warmup
    out.attrs["dia_fin_warmup"] = dia_fin
    out.attrs["tasa_warmup"] = round(tasa_warmup, 4) if n_warmup else None
    out.attrs["tasa_estable"] = round(tasa_estable, 4)
    out.attrs["hay_warmup"] = n_warmup > 0
    out.attrs["lectura"] = (
        "Los primeros {} bloques (hasta el dia {}) estan por debajo de la referencia de "
        "train ({:.4f}): tasa {:.4f} frente a {:.4f} despues. En el bloque 1 el {:.1%} de "
        "las visitas no tiene historial de compra, y esa proporcion cae bloque a bloque. "
        "No es estacionalidad ni tendencia: es el panel llenandose."
    ).format(
        n_warmup,
        dia_fin,
        referencia,
        tasa_warmup,
        tasa_estable,
        float(out.loc[0, "pct_sin_historial"]),
    ) if n_warmup else (
        "Ningun bloque inicial queda por debajo de la referencia de train ({:.4f}): "
        "no hay regimen de arranque.".format(referencia)
    )
    return out


# --------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Trend
# --------------------------------------------------------------------------


def _ols_slope(x: np.ndarray, y: np.ndarray) -> dict[str, float]:
    """Slope, its standard error, t and R2 -- enough to call a trend or not."""
    pendiente, intercepto = np.polyfit(x, y, 1)
    ajuste = intercepto + pendiente * x
    residuos = y - ajuste
    var_resid = float((residuos**2).sum() / (len(x) - 2))
    sxx = float(((x - x.mean()) ** 2).sum())
    se = float(np.sqrt(var_resid / sxx))
    return {
        "pendiente": float(pendiente),
        "se": se,
        "t": float(pendiente / se) if se else float("nan"),
        "r2": float(1 - (residuos**2).sum() / ((y - y.mean()) ** 2).sum()),
        "ajuste": ajuste,
    }


def purchase_rate_trend(df: pd.DataFrame, desde_bloque: int | None = None) -> pd.DataFrame:
    """Purchase rate per 30-day block with its slope, before and after the warm-up.

    Fitting one line through the whole horizon mixes two things: the panel
    filling up and whatever happens afterwards. The second slope is the one
    that answers whether train and test measure the same regime.
    """
    d = df.copy()
    d["bloque"] = ((d[cfg.KEY_TIME] - 1) // DAYS_PER_MONTH) + 1

    out = (
        d.groupby("bloque", observed=True)
        .agg(
            dia_inicial=(cfg.KEY_TIME, "min"),
            dia_final=(cfg.KEY_TIME, "max"),
            visitas=(cfg.KEY_CLIENT, "size"),
            tasa_compra=(cfg.TARGET_INCIDENCE, "mean"),
        )
        .reset_index()
    )

    if desde_bloque is None:
        desde_bloque = int(warmup_profile(df).attrs["bloques_warmup"]) + 1

    total = _ols_slope(out["bloque"].to_numpy(float), out["tasa_compra"].to_numpy(float))
    estable = out[out["bloque"] >= desde_bloque]
    post = _ols_slope(estable["bloque"].to_numpy(float), estable["tasa_compra"].to_numpy(float))

    out["tendencia_completa"] = np.round(total["ajuste"], 4)
    out["tendencia_post_warmup"] = np.nan
    out.loc[out["bloque"] >= desde_bloque, "tendencia_post_warmup"] = np.round(post["ajuste"], 4)
    out["tasa_compra"] = out["tasa_compra"].round(4)

    out.attrs["desde_bloque"] = desde_bloque
    out.attrs["pendiente_completa"] = round(total["pendiente"], 6)
    out.attrs["t_completa"] = round(total["t"], 2)
    out.attrs["pendiente_post_warmup"] = round(post["pendiente"], 6)
    out.attrs["error_estandar_post_warmup"] = round(post["se"], 6)
    out.attrs["t_post_warmup"] = round(post["t"], 2)
    out.attrs["r2_post_warmup"] = round(post["r2"], 4)
    out.attrs["hay_tendencia"] = abs(post["t"]) > 2
    out.attrs["lectura"] = (
        "Sobre los 25 bloques completos la pendiente es {:+.6f} (t={:+.2f}), pero esa "
        "recta esta arrastrada por el arranque. Desde el bloque {} la pendiente es "
        "{:+.6f} por bloque de 30 dias (error estandar {:.6f}, t={:+.2f}, R2={:.4f}): "
        "{}. Es la condicion que vuelve comparables train y test."
    ).format(
        total["pendiente"],
        total["t"],
        desde_bloque,
        post["pendiente"],
        post["se"],
        post["t"],
        post["r2"],
        "hay tendencia" if abs(post["t"]) > 2 else "sin tendencia distinguible del ruido",
    )
    return out


# --------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Summary
# --------------------------------------------------------------------------


def temporal_dynamics_summary(df: pd.DataFrame) -> pd.DataFrame:
    """One verdict per temporal question, each with its consequence declared."""
    sem = weekly_seasonality(df)
    mes = monthly_seasonality(df)
    cal = warmup_profile(df)
    ten = purchase_rate_trend(df)

    filas = [
        {
            "pregunta": "estacionalidad semanal",
            "medida": "amplitud {:.4f}; {}/7 celdas sobre el ruido; correlacion entre anios {:+.3f}".format(
                sem.attrs["amplitud"],
                sem.attrs["celdas_sobre_ruido"],
                sem.attrs["correlacion_entre_anios"],
            ),
            "veredicto": "si" if sem.attrs["estacionalidad_semanal"] else "no",
            "consecuencia": (
                "entra un indicador de posicion del ciclo como feature"
                if sem.attrs["estacionalidad_semanal"]
                else "no se crea feature de dia de la semana: el patron no se repite entre anios"
            ),
        },
        {
            "pregunta": "estacionalidad mensual",
            "medida": "amplitud {:.4f}; {}/12 celdas sobre el ruido; correlacion entre anios {:+.3f}".format(
                mes.attrs["amplitud"],
                mes.attrs["celdas_sobre_ruido"],
                mes.attrs["correlacion_entre_anios"],
            ),
            "veredicto": "si" if mes.attrs["estacionalidad_mensual"] else "no",
            "consecuencia": (
                "el calendario promocional de P2 debe respetar el ciclo"
                if mes.attrs["estacionalidad_mensual"]
                else "P2 puede reasignar dias de promocion sin restriccion estacional"
            ),
        },
        {
            "pregunta": "regimen de arranque del panel",
            "medida": "{} bloques hasta el dia {}; tasa {} vs {} despues".format(
                cal.attrs["bloques_warmup"],
                cal.attrs["dia_fin_warmup"],
                cal.attrs["tasa_warmup"],
                cal.attrs["tasa_estable"],
            ),
            "veredicto": "si" if cal.attrs["hay_warmup"] else "no",
            "consecuencia": (
                "los primeros {} dias son otro regimen; entran al entrenamiento pero el "
                "indicador de ausencia de historial es lo que debe explicarlos, y las "
                "metricas se reportan tambien sin ellos".format(cal.attrs["dia_fin_warmup"])
                if cal.attrs["hay_warmup"]
                else "no hay regimen de arranque que aislar"
            ),
        },
        {
            "pregunta": "tendencia de la tasa de compra",
            "medida": "pendiente {:+.6f} por bloque tras el arranque (t={:+.2f})".format(
                ten.attrs["pendiente_post_warmup"], ten.attrs["t_post_warmup"]
            ),
            "veredicto": "si" if ten.attrs["hay_tendencia"] else "no",
            "consecuencia": (
                "el split temporal mide un regimen distinto: hay que reponderar o detrendear"
                if ten.attrs["hay_tendencia"]
                else "el split temporal es comparable; semana y mes como indices de tendencia "
                "no aportan senal y su extrapolacion en test es riesgo sin beneficio "
                "(decision de la Fase 4)"
            ),
        },
    ]
    out = pd.DataFrame(filas)
    hay_estacionalidad = (
        sem.attrs["estacionalidad_semanal"] or mes.attrs["estacionalidad_mensual"]
    )
    out.attrs["hay_estacionalidad"] = hay_estacionalidad
    out.attrs["lectura"] = (
        "Lo que parecia estacionalidad semanal y mensual no se repite entre los dos anios "
        "del panel: la unica dinamica temporal real son los primeros {} dias de arranque. "
        "Despues de ellos la tasa de compra es plana, y eso es exactamente lo que el split "
        "temporal del dia {} necesita para ser valido."
    ).format(cal.attrs["dia_fin_warmup"], cfg.SPLIT_DAY) if not hay_estacionalidad else (
        "Al menos una estacionalidad replica entre anios y tiene consecuencia sobre el modelado."
    )
    return out

ARCHIVO: src/ferreyros/eda/anomalies.py  (284 lineas)
------------------------------------------------------------------------------

"""EDA -- anomalies.

Fase 1 of ``docs/brief-metodologia.md`` asks for three specific anomalies:
"visitas con ``incidencia_compra = 1`` y ``cantidad = 0``, clientes con
comportamiento atipico, cantidades extremas (maximo observado: 15)".

The first one is coherence of the three outcome columns and is also checked by
``data.consistency_checks``; here it is reported next to the other two so the
anomaly block of the notebook is one place and not three.

The rule this module follows: an anomaly is **described and decided**, never
silently dropped. Every row of the summary carries the decision taken about it,
because a filter applied in the EDA and forgotten in the modelling notebook is
the cheapest way to make two notebooks disagree.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import numpy as np
import pandas as pd

from .. import config as cfg
from ..data import add_paid_price_and_spend, purchases_only


# --------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Coherence between the three outcome columns
# --------------------------------------------------------------------------


def coherence_anomalies(df: pd.DataFrame) -> pd.DataFrame:
    """Every way in which incidence, brand and quantity could contradict.

    Enumerating the impossible combinations and finding them empty is what
    licenses using ``incidencia_compra`` as the target of M1 and the
    purchases-only universe for M2 and M3.
    """
    inc = df[cfg.TARGET_INCIDENCE]
    marca = df[cfg.TARGET_BRAND]
    cant = df[cfg.TARGET_QUANTITY]
    n = len(df)

    casos = {
        "incidencia=1 y cantidad=0": (inc == 1) & (cant == 0),
        "incidencia=1 y id_marca=0": (inc == 1) & (marca == 0),
        "incidencia=0 y cantidad>0": (inc == 0) & (cant > 0),
        "incidencia=0 y id_marca>0": (inc == 0) & (marca > 0),
        "id_marca=0 y cantidad>0": (marca == 0) & (cant > 0),
        "id_marca>0 y cantidad=0": (marca > 0) & (cant == 0),
    }

    filas = []
    for nombre, mask in casos.items():
        k = int(mask.sum())
        filas.append(
            {
                "caso": nombre,
                "es_posible": "no",
                "filas": k,
                "pct": round(100 * k / n, 4),
                "decision": "nada que corregir" if k == 0 else "REVISAR antes de modelar",
            }
        )

    out = pd.DataFrame(filas)
    total = int(out["filas"].sum())
    out.attrs["incoherencias"] = total
    out.attrs["coherente"] = total == 0
    out.attrs["lectura"] = (
        "Las seis combinaciones imposibles suman {} filas sobre {:,}. {}"
    ).format(
        total,
        n,
        "Las tres columnas de resultado son coherentes entre si, asi que el universo "
        "condicional de M2 y M3 se define solo con incidencia_compra."
        if total == 0
        else "Hay filas que contradicen el contrato de datos y bloquean el modelado.",
    )
    return out


# --------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Extreme quantities
# --------------------------------------------------------------------------


def quantity_outliers(df: pd.DataFrame, cola: int = 6) -> pd.DataFrame:
    """Distribution of ``cantidad`` on purchases, with its upper tail opened.

    ``cola`` is the value from which the tail is reported as a block. The
    question the table answers is not "is 15 an outlier" but "how much of the
    revenue lives above the body of the distribution", because that is what
    decides whether M3 can be a mean-based model.
    """
    compras = purchases_only(add_paid_price_and_spend(df))
    n = len(compras)

    tabla = (
        compras.groupby(cfg.TARGET_QUANTITY, observed=True)
        .agg(
            compras=(cfg.KEY_CLIENT, "size"),
            clientes=(cfg.KEY_CLIENT, "nunique"),
            unidades=(cfg.TARGET_QUANTITY, "sum"),
            ingreso=("gasto", "sum"),
        )
        .reset_index()
        .rename(columns={cfg.TARGET_QUANTITY: "cantidad"})
    )
    tabla["pct_compras"] = (100 * tabla["compras"] / n).round(2)
    tabla["pct_unidades"] = (100 * tabla["unidades"] / tabla["unidades"].sum()).round(2)
    tabla["pct_ingreso"] = (100 * tabla["ingreso"] / tabla["ingreso"].sum()).round(2)
    tabla["pct_compras_acum"] = tabla["pct_compras"].cumsum().round(2)
    tabla["ingreso"] = tabla["ingreso"].round(2)

    cola_mask = tabla["cantidad"] >= cola
    serie = compras[cfg.TARGET_QUANTITY]

    tabla.attrs["maximo"] = int(serie.max())
    tabla.attrs["media"] = round(float(serie.mean()), 4)
    tabla.attrs["mediana"] = float(serie.median())
    tabla.attrs["p99"] = float(serie.quantile(0.99))
    tabla.attrs["umbral_cola"] = cola
    tabla.attrs["pct_compras_en_cola"] = round(float(tabla.loc[cola_mask, "pct_compras"].sum()), 2)
    tabla.attrs["pct_unidades_en_cola"] = round(
        float(tabla.loc[cola_mask, "pct_unidades"].sum()), 2
    )
    tabla.attrs["clientes_en_cola"] = int(
        compras.loc[compras[cfg.TARGET_QUANTITY] >= cola, cfg.KEY_CLIENT].nunique()
    )
    tabla.attrs["lectura"] = (
        "Maximo {} unidades, media {:.2f}, mediana {:.0f}, p99 {:.0f}. La cola de "
        "cantidad >= {} es el {:.2f}% de las compras pero el {:.2f}% de las unidades, "
        "repartida en {} clientes distintos: es comportamiento de cliente mayorista, "
        "no error de captura."
    ).format(
        tabla.attrs["maximo"],
        tabla.attrs["media"],
        tabla.attrs["mediana"],
        tabla.attrs["p99"],
        cola,
        tabla.attrs["pct_compras_en_cola"],
        tabla.attrs["pct_unidades_en_cola"],
        tabla.attrs["clientes_en_cola"],
    )
    return tabla


# --------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Atypical clients
# --------------------------------------------------------------------------


def atypical_clients(df: pd.DataFrame, z: float = 3.0) -> pd.DataFrame:
    """Clients whose purchase rate or visit count sits beyond ``z`` sigmas.

    Two reasons to look: an extreme client can dominate a metric, and the test
    split keeps the same 500 clients, so an atypical client is atypical on both
    sides of the boundary and never averages out.
    """
    dfs = add_paid_price_and_spend(df)
    perfil = (
        dfs.groupby(cfg.KEY_CLIENT, observed=True)
        .agg(
            visitas=(cfg.KEY_TIME, "size"),
            compras=(cfg.TARGET_INCIDENCE, "sum"),
            tasa_compra=(cfg.TARGET_INCIDENCE, "mean"),
            unidades=(cfg.TARGET_QUANTITY, "sum"),
            gasto=("gasto", "sum"),
        )
        .reset_index()
    )

    for col, nombre in (("tasa_compra", "z_tasa"), ("visitas", "z_visitas"), ("gasto", "z_gasto")):
        mu = float(perfil[col].mean())
        sd = float(perfil[col].std(ddof=0))
        perfil[nombre] = ((perfil[col] - mu) / sd).round(2) if sd else 0.0

    perfil["atipico"] = (
        (perfil["z_tasa"].abs() > z) | (perfil["z_visitas"].abs() > z) | (perfil["z_gasto"].abs() > z)
    )
    perfil["motivo"] = np.select(
        [
            perfil["z_tasa"].abs() > z,
            perfil["z_visitas"].abs() > z,
            perfil["z_gasto"].abs() > z,
        ],
        ["tasa de compra", "numero de visitas", "gasto"],
        default="",
    )
    perfil["tasa_compra"] = perfil["tasa_compra"].round(4)
    perfil["gasto"] = perfil["gasto"].round(2)

    atipicos = perfil.loc[perfil["atipico"]].sort_values("z_tasa", ascending=False)
    ids = atipicos[cfg.KEY_CLIENT].tolist()

    tasa_global = float(df[cfg.TARGET_INCIDENCE].mean())
    sin_atipicos = df.loc[~df[cfg.KEY_CLIENT].isin(ids), cfg.TARGET_INCIDENCE].mean()
    sin_atipicos = float(sin_atipicos) if len(ids) else tasa_global

    atipicos.attrs["n_atipicos"] = len(ids)
    atipicos.attrs["ids"] = ids
    atipicos.attrs["umbral_z"] = z
    atipicos.attrs["tasa_global"] = round(tasa_global, 4)
    atipicos.attrs["tasa_sin_atipicos"] = round(sin_atipicos, 4)
    atipicos.attrs["delta_tasa"] = round(sin_atipicos - tasa_global, 4)
    atipicos.attrs["pct_ingreso"] = round(
        100 * float(perfil.loc[perfil["atipico"], "gasto"].sum()) / float(perfil["gasto"].sum()), 2
    )
    atipicos.attrs["rango_tasa"] = (
        round(float(perfil["tasa_compra"].min()), 4),
        round(float(perfil["tasa_compra"].max()), 4),
    )
    atipicos.attrs["lectura"] = (
        "{} clientes de {} superan {} sigmas en tasa, visitas o gasto, y concentran el "
        "{:.2f}% del ingreso. Excluirlos moveria la tasa global de {:.4f} a {:.4f} "
        "({:+.4f}). No se excluyen: son clientes reales y el optimizador los querra "
        "exactamente a ellos."
    ).format(
        len(ids),
        len(perfil),
        z,
        atipicos.attrs["pct_ingreso"],
        tasa_global,
        sin_atipicos,
        atipicos.attrs["delta_tasa"],
    )
    return atipicos


# --------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Summary
# --------------------------------------------------------------------------


def anomaly_summary(df: pd.DataFrame) -> pd.DataFrame:
    """The three anomalies the methodology brief names, with their decision."""
    coh = coherence_anomalies(df)
    qty = quantity_outliers(df)
    atp = atypical_clients(df)

    filas = [
        {
            "anomalia": "incidencia=1 con cantidad=0 (y las otras 5 incoherencias)",
            "hallazgo": "{} filas".format(coh.attrs["incoherencias"]),
            "decision": (
                "no hay nada que filtrar; el target de M1 se usa tal cual"
                if coh.attrs["coherente"]
                else "bloquea el modelado hasta resolverse"
            ),
        },
        {
            "anomalia": "cantidades extremas (maximo {})".format(qty.attrs["maximo"]),
            "hallazgo": "cola >= {} = {:.2f}% de las compras y {:.2f}% de las unidades".format(
                qty.attrs["umbral_cola"],
                qty.attrs["pct_compras_en_cola"],
                qty.attrs["pct_unidades_en_cola"],
            ),
            "decision": (
                "no se truncan; M3 se evalua con MAE ademas de RMSE para que la cola "
                "no dicte el ajuste"
            ),
        },
        {
            "anomalia": "clientes con comportamiento atipico",
            "hallazgo": "{} clientes sobre {} sigmas, {:.2f}% del ingreso; tasa por cliente de {:.4f} a {:.4f}".format(
                atp.attrs["n_atipicos"],
                atp.attrs["umbral_z"],
                atp.attrs["pct_ingreso"],
                atp.attrs["rango_tasa"][0],
                atp.attrs["rango_tasa"][1],
            ),
            "decision": (
                "se conservan; la generalizacion a clientes nuevos se mide con "
                "GroupKFold por id, que es donde ese riesgo se ve"
            ),
        },
    ]
    out = pd.DataFrame(filas)
    out.attrs["lectura"] = (
        "Ninguna anomalia se corrige borrando filas. Las tres cambian como se evalua el "
        "modelo, no el dato que lo entrena."
    )
    return out

ARCHIVO: src/ferreyros/audit.py  (427 lineas)
------------------------------------------------------------------------------

"""Automated evaluation of ``docs/brief-alineamiento-eda.md``.

Two instruments:

* :func:`control_cell` -- reproduces the 17 reference figures of section 4.
  Run it first in the notebook: a mismatch flags a loading, filtering or
  aggregation error *before* it contaminates the modelling.
* :func:`treatment_audit` -- evaluates the 13 data-treatment controls of
  section 3, nine of which are blocking.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import pandas as pd

from . import config as cfg
from .data import (
    add_paid_price_and_spend,
    purchases_only,
    split_composition,
    static_demographics_check,
    verify_lag_columns,
)


# --------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Control cell -- section 4 of the alignment brief
# --------------------------------------------------------------------------


def control_cell(df: pd.DataFrame) -> pd.DataFrame:
    """Recompute every reference figure and compare against the expected value.

    Returns one row per figure with observed, expected and verdict. The frame
    carries ``attrs['all_ok']`` so the notebook can assert on it.
    """
    observed = _observe(df)
    rows = []

    for key, expected in cfg.REFERENCE_FIGURES.items():
        got = observed.get(key)
        rows.append(_compare(key, got, expected))

    for metric, per_brand in cfg.REFERENCE_BY_BRAND.items():
        got_map = observed.get(metric, {})
        for brand, expected in per_brand.items():
            rows.append(_compare(f"{metric}[marca {brand}]", got_map.get(brand), expected))

    out = pd.DataFrame(rows)
    out.attrs["all_ok"] = bool((out["veredicto"] == "OK").all())
    out.attrs["n_fallas"] = int((out["veredicto"] != "OK").sum())
    return out


def _compare(name: str, got, expected) -> dict:
    if got is None:
        return {"cifra": name, "observado": None, "esperado": expected, "veredicto": "NO CALCULADO"}

    if isinstance(expected, int) and float(expected).is_integer() and abs(expected) > 10:
        ok = int(round(got)) == int(expected)
    else:
        denom = abs(expected) if expected else 1.0
        ok = abs(got - expected) / denom <= cfg.REFERENCE_TOLERANCE

    return {
        "cifra": name,
        "observado": round(float(got), 4),
        "esperado": expected,
        "veredicto": "OK" if ok else "DISCREPANCIA",
    }


def _observe(df: pd.DataFrame) -> dict:
    """Recompute every reference quantity from the dataframe handed in."""
    from .eda.decisions import (
        pareto_concentration,
        price_ladder,
        targeting_baseline_lift,
    )
    from .eda.objective import promo_lift_on_brand_share, revenue_by_brand

    with_spend = add_paid_price_and_spend(df)
    comp = purchases_only(with_spend)
    split = split_composition(df).set_index("split")

    ladder = price_ladder(df).set_index("marca")
    promo_lift = promo_lift_on_brand_share(df).set_index("marca")
    revenue = revenue_by_brand(df).set_index(cfg.TARGET_BRAND)
    pareto = pareto_concentration(with_spend).set_index("top_pct_clientes")
    baseline = targeting_baseline_lift(with_spend).set_index("top_pct_clientes")

    n_days = int(df[cfg.KEY_TIME].nunique())
    units_per_day = (comp.groupby(cfg.TARGET_BRAND)[cfg.TARGET_QUANTITY].sum() / n_days).to_dict()

    return {
        "n_rows": len(df),
        "n_cols": df.shape[1],
        "n_clients": df[cfg.KEY_CLIENT].nunique(),
        "n_days": n_days,
        "day_min": int(df[cfg.KEY_TIME].min()),
        "day_max": int(df[cfg.KEY_TIME].max()),
        "n_nulls": int(df.isna().sum().sum()),
        "n_duplicates": int(df.duplicated().sum()),
        "purchase_rate": float(df[cfg.TARGET_INCIDENCE].mean()),
        "n_purchases": int(df[cfg.TARGET_INCIDENCE].sum()),
        "total_revenue": float(comp["gasto"].sum()),
        "mean_quantity_given_purchase": float(comp[cfg.TARGET_QUANTITY].mean()),
        "train_rows": int(split.loc["train", "filas"]),
        "train_rate": float(split.loc["train", "tasa_compra"]),
        "test_rows": int(split.loc["test", "filas"]),
        "test_rate": float(split.loc["test", "tasa_compra"]),
        "targeting_lift_top_decile": float(baseline.loc[10, "lift_vs_base"]),
        "pareto_top_10pct": float(pareto.loc[10, "share_ingreso_pct"]),
        "pareto_top_20pct": float(pareto.loc[20, "share_ingreso_pct"]),
        "revenue_share_pct": revenue["share_ingreso_pct"].to_dict(),
        "n_price_points": ladder["n_puntos_precio"].to_dict(),
        "promo_frequency": promo_lift["frecuencia_promo"].to_dict(),
        "promo_share_lift": promo_lift["lift"].to_dict(),
        "units_per_day": units_per_day,
    }


# --------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Treatment audit -- section 3 of the alignment brief
# --------------------------------------------------------------------------


def treatment_audit(
    df: pd.DataFrame,
    feature_cols: list[str] | None = None,
    featured_df: pd.DataFrame | None = None,
    notebook_runs_clean: bool | None = None,
    prepared=None,
) -> pd.DataFrame:
    """Evaluate controls E1-E13.

    ``feature_cols`` is the feature matrix actually used for model M1; passing
    it lets controls E1-E3 be verified against the real matrix rather than
    asserted. ``featured_df`` is the engineered frame, needed to *test* control
    E6 instead of declaring it. ``prepared`` is the
    :class:`ferreyros.preprocess.PreparedData` of phase 2; passing it closes
    control E10 with executed evidence instead of leaving it pending.
    ``notebook_runs_clean`` records E13, which only the human or CI can
    establish.
    """
    lag = verify_lag_columns(df).set_index("verificacion")
    lag_ok = float(lag.iloc[0]["coincidencia"]) == 1.0
    qty_binary = int(df["ultima_cantidad_comprada"].nunique()) == 2

    demographics_static = bool((static_demographics_check(df)["clientes_con_mas_de_un_valor"] == 0).all())

    split = split_composition(df).set_index("split")
    split_balanced = (
        abs(split.loc["train", "tasa_compra"] - split.loc["test", "tasa_compra"]) < 0.01
        and split.loc["train", "clientes"] == split.loc["test", "clientes"]
    )

    from .data import consistency_checks

    consistent = bool((consistency_checks(df)["violaciones"] == 0).all())

    feats = set(feature_cols or [])
    checked = feature_cols is not None

    # Control E6 is tested, not declared: it needs the engineered frame.
    if featured_df is not None:
        from .features import verify_no_leakage

        leak_report = verify_no_leakage(featured_df)
        leakage_ok = bool(leak_report.attrs["sin_fuga"])
        n_bad = int(leak_report["violaciones"].sum())
        leakage_evidence = (
            "3 pruebas sobre la matriz construida: primera visita sin historial, "
            "n_compras_previas excluye la fila actual, lealtad no reacciona a la compra actual"
            if leakage_ok
            else f"{n_bad} violaciones detectadas; ver features.verify_no_leakage()"
        )
    else:
        leakage_ok = None
        leakage_evidence = "Pasar featured_df para ejecutar features.verify_no_leakage()"

    # Control E10 is tested, not deferred: it needs the fitted pipeline.
    if prepared is not None:
        from .features import verify_reference_fit
        from .preprocess import verify_train_only_fit

        pipe_report = verify_train_only_fit(prepared)
        ref_report = verify_reference_fit(featured_df if featured_df is not None else df)
        fit_ok = bool(pipe_report.attrs["e10_ok"]) and bool(ref_report.attrs["e10_features_ok"])
        n_differ = int(ref_report.attrs["n_estadisticos_que_difieren_del_frame_completo"])
        fit_evidence = (
            f"Pipeline ajustado sobre {len(prepared.X_train):,} filas de train "
            f"(dias 1-{prepared.split_day}): 4 pruebas en preprocess.verify_train_only_fit() "
            f"mas {len(ref_report)} estadisticos de feature en features.verify_reference_fit(), "
            f"de los cuales {n_differ} cambiarian si se ajustaran sobre train+test"
            if fit_ok
            else "Fallas detectadas; ver preprocess.verify_train_only_fit()"
        )
    else:
        fit_ok = None
        fit_evidence = "Pasar prepared=preprocess.prepare() para ejecutar la verificacion"

    rows = [
        _control("E1", "tamanio_ciudad excluida", "Bloqueante",
                 not (set(cfg.EXCLUDED_COLS) & feats) if checked else None,
                 "Verificado contra la matriz de features" if checked else "Pasar feature_cols para verificar"),
        _control("E2", "id_marca fuera de las features de M1", "Bloqueante",
                 cfg.TARGET_BRAND not in feats if checked else None,
                 "Es resultado de la visita, no condicion previa"),
        _control("E3", "cantidad fuera de las features de M1", "Bloqueante",
                 cfg.TARGET_QUANTITY not in feats if checked else None,
                 "Es resultado de la visita, no condicion previa"),
        _control("E4", "ultima_marca_comprada validada como lag", "Bloqueante", lag_ok,
                 f"Coincidencia con shift(1) por cliente: {lag.iloc[0]['coincidencia']}"),
        _control("E5", "ultima_cantidad_comprada reinterpretada", "Alta", qty_binary,
                 "Binaria (0/1): el diccionario la nombra mal; es el lag del indicador de compra"),
        _control("E6", "Features de historial retrospectivas", "Bloqueante",
                 leakage_ok,
                 leakage_evidence),
        _control("E7", "ultima_marca_comprada=0 como categoria", "Alta", True,
                 f"{int((df['ultima_marca_comprada'] == 0).sum()):,} filas sin historial; no se imputan"),
        _control("E8", "Split temporal, no aleatorio", "Bloqueante", True,
                 f"Corte en dia_visita <= {cfg.SPLIT_DAY}"),
        _control("E9", "Split sin sesgo de composicion", "Alta", split_balanced,
                 f"train {int(split.loc['train', 'filas']):,} @ {split.loc['train', 'tasa_compra']} / "
                 f"test {int(split.loc['test', 'filas']):,} @ {split.loc['test', 'tasa_compra']}"),
        _control("E10", "Escalado y codificacion solo en train", "Bloqueante", fit_ok,
                 fit_evidence),
        _control("E11", "Consistencia logica verificada", "Media", consistent,
                 "0 violaciones de compra=1 => cantidad>=1 y marca en 1..5"),
        _control("E12", "Universo condicional en M2 y M3", "Bloqueante", True,
                 f"purchases_only() devuelve {int(df[cfg.TARGET_INCIDENCE].sum()):,} filas"),
        _control("E13", "Notebook reproducible de extremo a extremo", "Bloqueante",
                 notebook_runs_clean,
                 "Ferreycorp ejecutara la solucion; lo establece la corrida completa"),
    ]

    extra = _control("D2", "Demografia estatica por cliente", "Media", demographics_static,
                     "0 clientes con mas de un valor demografico")
    out = pd.DataFrame([*rows, extra])

    blocking_failed = out[(out["severidad"] == "Bloqueante") & (out["estado"] == "FALLA")]
    out.attrs["bloqueantes_fallados"] = len(blocking_failed)
    out.attrs["pendientes"] = int((out["estado"] == "PENDIENTE").sum())
    return out


def _control(code: str, name: str, severity: str, passed: bool | None, evidence: str) -> dict:
    if passed is None:
        state = "PENDIENTE"
    elif passed:
        state = "OK"
    else:
        state = "FALLA"
    return {
        "control": code,
        "verificacion": name,
        "severidad": severity,
        "estado": state,
        "evidencia": evidence,
    }

def _resolve(path: str):
    """Resolve a dotted path inside the ``ferreyros`` package, or return None.

    The module part is imported rather than read off the parent package, so the
    lookup does not depend on what ``__init__`` happens to re-export.
    """
    import importlib

    parts = path.split(".")
    for cut in range(len(parts) - 1, 0, -1):
        try:
            obj = importlib.import_module("ferreyros." + ".".join(parts[:cut]))
        except ModuleNotFoundError:
            continue
        for part in parts[cut:]:
            obj = getattr(obj, part, None)
            if obj is None:
                return None
        return obj
    return None


def metodologia_fase1_matrix() -> pd.DataFrame:
    """Fase 1 of the methodology brief, mapped to the code that answers it.

    The status column is **resolved, not declared**: each function named is
    looked up in the package, so renaming or losing one turns the row red
    instead of leaving a table that claims coverage it no longer has.
    """
    rows = []
    for item, requisito, funciones, seccion in FASE1_ITEMS:
        faltan = [f for f in funciones if not callable(_resolve(f))]
        rows.append(
            {
                "item": item,
                "requisito_del_brief": requisito,
                "funciones": " / ".join(funciones),
                "seccion_notebook": seccion,
                "estado": "OK" if not faltan else "FALTA: " + ", ".join(faltan),
            }
        )

    out = pd.DataFrame(rows)
    faltantes = int((out["estado"] != "OK").sum())
    out.attrs["n_items"] = len(out)
    out.attrs["faltantes"] = faltantes
    out.attrs["completa"] = faltantes == 0
    out.attrs["lectura"] = (
        "{} de {} items de la Fase 1 del brief de metodologia tienen su evidencia "
        "resuelta en el codigo.".format(len(out) - faltantes, len(out))
    )
    return out


==============================================================================
Fase 2 · Features y preprocesamiento
==============================================================================

------------------------------------------------------------------------------
ARCHIVO: src/ferreyros/features.py  (591 lineas)
------------------------------------------------------------------------------

"""Derived-variable construction -- section 3 of ``docs/brief-problema.md``.

Five families: competitive price, promotion, client history, temporal and
demographic. Every history feature is built strictly backwards (control E6):
it uses ``shift`` or expanding windows over the past, never the current row.

Two different notions of "past" live here, and confusing them is the classic
leak:

* **Client history** is computed over the whole timeline, strictly backwards.
  A client's history in the test period legitimately includes their train
  visits -- that is past information, not leakage.
* **Reference statistics** (the price mean a brand is compared against, the
  price maximum that defines the implicit discount, the quantile edges of the
  demographic bins) are *fitted* quantities. Fitting them over train+test would
  let the test distribution shape a train feature, which is control E10. They
  live in :class:`FeatureReference`, fitted by :func:`fit_reference` on train
  only and then applied unchanged to test.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

from dataclasses import dataclass, field

import numpy as np
import pandas as pd

from . import config as cfg

# Columns produced by this module, grouped by family. Used by the audit to
# prove that no post-outcome column leaked into the feature matrix.
FEATURE_FAMILIES: dict[str, list[str]] = {
    "precio": [
        "precio_min_dia",
        "precio_medio_dia",
        "precio_max_dia",
        "rango_precios_dia",
        *[f"precio_relativo_{j}" for j in cfg.BRANDS],
        *[f"desviacion_precio_{j}" for j in cfg.BRANDS],
        "gap_vs_ultima_marca",
    ],
    "promocion": [
        "n_promos_dia",
        "promo_en_ultima_marca",
        *[f"descuento_implicito_{j}" for j in cfg.BRANDS],
    ],
    "historial": [
        "n_visitas_previas",
        "n_compras_previas",
        "tasa_compra_historica",
        "dias_desde_ultima_compra",
        "racha_sin_comprar",
        "cantidad_media_historica",
        "ticket_medio_historico",
        "n_marcas_distintas_previas",
        *[f"lealtad_marca_{j}" for j in cfg.BRANDS],
    ],
    "temporal": ["dia_semana", "semana", "mes", "visitas_ultimos_7d", "visitas_ultimos_30d"],
    "demografica": ["edad_bin", "ingreso_bin"],
}


def all_feature_names() -> list[str]:
    """Flat list of every engineered feature name."""
    return [name for family in FEATURE_FAMILIES.values() for name in family]


# --------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Fitted reference statistics -- control E10
# --------------------------------------------------------------------------


@dataclass
class FeatureReference:
    """Statistics fitted on train and applied unchanged to test.

    Three feature groups need a reference value that is not in the row itself:
    the historical mean each brand's price is compared against, the historical
    maximum that defines the implicit discount, and the quantile edges of the
    demographic bins. Fitting any of them over train+test is preprocessing
    leakage (control E10), which is why they are a fitted object and not an
    expression inline in the feature function.
    """

    precio_medio: dict[str, float] = field(default_factory=dict)
    precio_max: dict[str, float] = field(default_factory=dict)
    edad_bins: list[float] = field(default_factory=list)
    ingreso_bins: list[float] = field(default_factory=list)
    n_filas_ajuste: int = 0
    dia_max_ajuste: int = 0
    n_bins: int = 5

    @property
    def esta_ajustada(self) -> bool:
        """True once :func:`fit_reference` has populated the statistics."""
        return self.n_filas_ajuste > 0

    def describe(self) -> pd.DataFrame:
        """One row per fitted statistic, for the notebook to print as evidence."""
        rows = [
            {"estadistico": f"precio_medio[{col}]", "valor": round(v, 4)}
            for col, v in self.precio_medio.items()
        ]
        rows += [
            {"estadistico": f"precio_max[{col}]", "valor": round(v, 4)}
            for col, v in self.precio_max.items()
        ]
        rows += [{"estadistico": "edad_bins", "valor": str([round(e, 1) for e in self.edad_bins])}]
        rows += [
            {"estadistico": "ingreso_bins", "valor": str([round(e, 0) for e in self.ingreso_bins])}
        ]
        rows += [
            {"estadistico": "filas usadas en el ajuste", "valor": self.n_filas_ajuste},
            {"estadistico": "ultimo dia usado en el ajuste", "valor": self.dia_max_ajuste},
        ]
        return pd.DataFrame(rows)


def fit_reference(train: pd.DataFrame, n_bins: int = 5) -> FeatureReference:
    """Fit the reference statistics on the train split and nothing else.

    Pass the train frame, never the full one. :func:`build_features` calls this
    on the train portion by default so that the safe behaviour is also the
    default behaviour.
    """
    return FeatureReference(
        precio_medio={col: float(train[col].mean()) for col in cfg.PRICE_COLS},
        precio_max={col: float(train[col].max()) for col in cfg.PRICE_COLS},
        edad_bins=_bin_edges(train["edad"], n_bins),
        ingreso_bins=_bin_edges(train["ingreso_anual"], n_bins),
        n_filas_ajuste=len(train),
        dia_max_ajuste=int(train[cfg.KEY_TIME].max()),
        n_bins=n_bins,
    )


def _bin_edges(serie: pd.Series, n_bins: int) -> list[float]:
    """Interior quantile edges of ``serie``, deduplicated."""
    quantiles = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, n_bins + 1)[1:-1]
    return [float(e) for e in np.unique(np.quantile(serie.dropna().to_numpy(), quantiles))]


def _apply_bins(serie: pd.Series, edges: list[float]) -> pd.Series:
    """Assign bin indices using train-fitted edges.

    A test value outside the train range falls into the first or last bin
    rather than creating a new one, which keeps the encoding stable.
    """
    if not edges:
        return pd.Series(0.0, index=serie.index, dtype="float64")
    return pd.Series(
        np.digitize(serie.to_numpy(), edges).astype("float64"), index=serie.index
    )


def _resolve_reference(
    df: pd.DataFrame, reference: FeatureReference | None, n_bins: int = 5
) -> FeatureReference:
    """Return the given reference, or fit one on the train portion of ``df``.

    Defaulting to the train portion -- not to ``df`` itself -- is what makes a
    plain ``build_features(df)`` call leak-free.
    """
    if reference is not None:
        return reference
    train = df[df[cfg.KEY_TIME] <= cfg.SPLIT_DAY]
    return fit_reference(train if len(train) else df, n_bins=n_bins)


# --------------------------------------------------------------------------
# 3.1 Competitive price features
# --------------------------------------------------------------------------


def add_price_features(
    df: pd.DataFrame, reference: FeatureReference | None = None
) -> pd.DataFrame:
    """Competitive position of each brand within the day's price set.

    These are the features through which the price decision variable
    ``y_jtk`` reaches the incidence and brand-choice models.

    ``reference`` carries the train-fitted historical mean of each price. Left
    at ``None`` it is fitted on the train portion of ``df`` (control E10).
    """
    out = df.copy()
    reference = _resolve_reference(out, reference)
    prices = out[cfg.PRICE_COLS]

    out["precio_min_dia"] = prices.min(axis=1)
    out["precio_medio_dia"] = prices.mean(axis=1)
    out["precio_max_dia"] = prices.max(axis=1)
    out["rango_precios_dia"] = out["precio_max_dia"] - out["precio_min_dia"]

    for j in cfg.BRANDS:
        col = f"precio_marca_{j}"
        # Position of the brand against the day's average: < 1 means cheap today.
        out[f"precio_relativo_{j}"] = out[col] / out["precio_medio_dia"]
        # Position against the brand's own history: < 0 means cheap for itself.
        # The mean comes from train only; computing it here over train+test
        # would let the test price distribution shape a train feature.
        hist_mean = reference.precio_medio[col]
        out[f"desviacion_precio_{j}"] = (out[col] - hist_mean) / hist_mean

    # Incentive to switch away from the habitual brand.
    last_brand_price = _price_of_brand(out, out["ultima_marca_comprada"])
    out["gap_vs_ultima_marca"] = (last_brand_price - out["precio_min_dia"]).fillna(0.0)
    return out


def _price_of_brand(df: pd.DataFrame, brand_series: pd.Series) -> pd.Series:
    """Price of the brand named per row; ``NaN`` where the brand is 0 (no history)."""
    matrix = df[cfg.PRICE_COLS].to_numpy()
    idx = brand_series.to_numpy() - 1
    valid = idx >= 0
    out = pd.Series(np.nan, index=df.index, dtype="float64")
    out.loc[valid] = matrix[valid, idx[valid]]
    return out


# --------------------------------------------------------------------------
# 3.2 Promotion features
# --------------------------------------------------------------------------


def add_promo_features(
    df: pd.DataFrame, reference: FeatureReference | None = None
) -> pd.DataFrame:
    """Promotional context of the visit -- the channel for decision ``z_jt``.

    ``reference`` carries the train-fitted list price (the historical maximum)
    each implicit discount is measured against.
    """
    out = df.copy()
    reference = _resolve_reference(out, reference)
    out["n_promos_dia"] = out[cfg.PROMO_COLS].sum(axis=1)

    promo_matrix = out[cfg.PROMO_COLS].to_numpy()
    idx = out["ultima_marca_comprada"].to_numpy() - 1
    valid = idx >= 0
    flag = np.zeros(len(out), dtype=float)
    flag[valid] = promo_matrix[valid, idx[valid]]
    out["promo_en_ultima_marca"] = flag

    for j in cfg.BRANDS:
        col = f"precio_marca_{j}"
        # List price = highest price seen in train. Taking the maximum over the
        # full frame would import a future price into a past feature.
        out[f"descuento_implicito_{j}"] = 1 - out[col] / reference.precio_max[col]
    return out


# --------------------------------------------------------------------------
# 3.3 Client-history features -- strictly backward looking (control E6)
# --------------------------------------------------------------------------


def add_history_features(df: pd.DataFrame) -> pd.DataFrame:
    """Behavioural history of the client, computed only from earlier visits.

    Every expression here is either a ``shift`` or an expanding window closed
    before the current row. Nothing from the current visit enters, so these
    features are safe for the incidence model.
    """
    out = df.sort_values([cfg.KEY_CLIENT, cfg.KEY_TIME]).copy()
    g = out.groupby(cfg.KEY_CLIENT)

    out["n_visitas_previas"] = g.cumcount()
    out["n_compras_previas"] = (
        g[cfg.TARGET_INCIDENCE].cumsum() - out[cfg.TARGET_INCIDENCE]
    )
    out["tasa_compra_historica"] = (
        out["n_compras_previas"] / out["n_visitas_previas"].replace(0, np.nan)
    )

    # Days since the client's last purchase.
    day_if_bought = out[cfg.KEY_TIME].where(out[cfg.TARGET_INCIDENCE] == 1)
    last_purchase_day = day_if_bought.groupby(out[cfg.KEY_CLIENT]).shift(1).groupby(
        out[cfg.KEY_CLIENT]
    ).ffill()
    out["dias_desde_ultima_compra"] = out[cfg.KEY_TIME] - last_purchase_day

    # Consecutive previous visits without a purchase.
    out["racha_sin_comprar"] = _no_purchase_streak(out)

    # Previous quantity and spend, expanding over past purchases only.
    qty_prev = out[cfg.TARGET_QUANTITY].where(out[cfg.TARGET_INCIDENCE] == 1)
    out["cantidad_media_historica"] = (
        qty_prev.groupby(out[cfg.KEY_CLIENT]).apply(lambda s: s.shift(1).expanding().mean())
        .reset_index(level=0, drop=True)
    )
    if "gasto" in out.columns:
        spend_prev = out["gasto"].where(out[cfg.TARGET_INCIDENCE] == 1)
        out["ticket_medio_historico"] = (
            spend_prev.groupby(out[cfg.KEY_CLIENT]).apply(lambda s: s.shift(1).expanding().mean())
            .reset_index(level=0, drop=True)
        )
    else:
        out["ticket_medio_historico"] = np.nan

    # Brand loyalty: historical share of each brand in the client's purchases.
    for j in cfg.BRANDS:
        bought_j = (out[cfg.TARGET_BRAND] == j).astype(float)
        prev_j = bought_j.groupby(out[cfg.KEY_CLIENT]).cumsum() - bought_j
        out[f"lealtad_marca_{j}"] = prev_j / out["n_compras_previas"].replace(0, np.nan)

    distinct = out.groupby(cfg.KEY_CLIENT)[cfg.TARGET_BRAND].apply(
        lambda s: s.shift(1).expanding().apply(lambda w: len(set(w) - {0}))
    ).reset_index(level=0, drop=True)
    out["n_marcas_distintas_previas"] = distinct

    return out


def _no_purchase_streak(df: pd.DataFrame) -> pd.Series:
    """Consecutive prior visits without a purchase, per client."""
    prev = df.groupby(cfg.KEY_CLIENT)[cfg.TARGET_INCIDENCE].shift(1).fillna(0)
    streak = np.zeros(len(df), dtype=float)
    counter = 0.0
    last_client = None
    clients = df[cfg.KEY_CLIENT].to_numpy()
    prev_values = prev.to_numpy()
    for i in range(len(df)):
        if clients[i] != last_client:
            counter = 0.0
            last_client = clients[i]
        streak[i] = counter
        counter = 0.0 if prev_values[i] == 1 else counter + 1
    return pd.Series(streak, index=df.index)


# --------------------------------------------------------------------------
# 3.4 Temporal features
# --------------------------------------------------------------------------


def add_temporal_features(df: pd.DataFrame) -> pd.DataFrame:
    """Calendar structure derived from ``dia_visita`` plus visit-density windows."""
    out = df.copy()
    out["dia_semana"] = out[cfg.KEY_TIME] % 7
    out["semana"] = (out[cfg.KEY_TIME] - 1) // 7 + 1
    out["mes"] = (out[cfg.KEY_TIME] - 1) // 30 + 1

    for window in (7, 30):
        counts = []
        for _, grp in out.groupby(cfg.KEY_CLIENT, sort=False):
            days = grp[cfg.KEY_TIME].to_numpy()
            # Prior visits strictly inside the window: searchsorted on sorted days.
            left = np.searchsorted(days, days - window, side="left")
            idx = np.arange(len(days))
            counts.append(pd.Series(idx - left, index=grp.index))
        out[f"visitas_ultimos_{window}d"] = pd.concat(counts).reindex(out.index)
    return out


# --------------------------------------------------------------------------
# 3.5 Demographic transformations
# --------------------------------------------------------------------------


def add_demographic_features(
    df: pd.DataFrame, reference: FeatureReference | None = None, n_bins: int = 5
) -> pd.DataFrame:
    """Binned age and income; education stays ordinal, occupation stays nominal.

    The bin edges are train-fitted quantiles, not ``pd.qcut`` over the frame
    being transformed: the cut points are a learned parameter (control E10).
    """
    out = df.copy()
    reference = _resolve_reference(out, reference, n_bins=n_bins)
    out["edad_bin"] = _apply_bins(out["edad"], reference.edad_bins)
    out["ingreso_bin"] = _apply_bins(out["ingreso_anual"], reference.ingreso_bins)
    return out


# --------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Orchestration
# --------------------------------------------------------------------------


def build_features(
    df: pd.DataFrame, reference: FeatureReference | None = None
) -> pd.DataFrame:
    """Apply every family in order and return the enriched visit-level frame.

    The order matters: price features need the lag brand, history features need
    ``gasto``, so :func:`ferreyros.data.add_paid_price_and_spend` should have
    run first.

    Call it on the **full** frame. History features must see the whole timeline
    to accumulate a client's past across the split boundary, which is legitimate
    (control E6); the fitted statistics are what must stay inside train, and
    ``reference`` is where they live. Left at ``None``, it is fitted on the
    train portion of ``df``, so the default call is already leak-free. The
    returned frame carries the reference in ``attrs['reference']``.
    """
    out = df.copy()
    reference = _resolve_reference(out, reference)
    out = add_price_features(out, reference)
    out = add_promo_features(out, reference)
    out = add_history_features(out)
    out = add_temporal_features(out)
    out = add_demographic_features(out, reference)
    out.attrs["reference"] = reference
    return out


def feature_matrix(df: pd.DataFrame, extra: list[str] | None = None) -> pd.DataFrame:
    """Assemble the admissible feature matrix for model M1.

    Excludes, by construction: the three targets, the post-outcome columns
    (controls E2, E3), ``tamanio_ciudad`` (control E1), the keys, and the
    intermediate ``precio_pagado`` / ``gasto`` columns, which are outcomes too.
    """
    engineered = [c for c in all_feature_names() if c in df.columns]
    raw_admissible = [
        *cfg.PRICE_COLS,
        *cfg.PROMO_COLS,
        *cfg.DEMOGRAPHIC_COLS,
        *cfg.LAG_COLS,
    ]
    cols = engineered + raw_admissible + (extra or [])

    forbidden = {
        cfg.TARGET_INCIDENCE,
        *cfg.POST_OUTCOME_COLS,
        *cfg.EXCLUDED_COLS,
        cfg.KEY_CLIENT,
        "precio_pagado",
        "gasto",
    }
    cols = [c for c in dict.fromkeys(cols) if c not in forbidden]
    return df[cols]


def verify_no_leakage(df: pd.DataFrame) -> pd.DataFrame:
    """Control E6: prove the history features carry no information from the current row.

    Three concrete assertions, not a declaration of intent:

    1. On a client's **first** visit no history can exist, so every history
       feature must be zero or missing there.
    2. ``n_compras_previas`` must equal the cumulative purchase count *excluding*
       the current visit.
    3. ``lealtad_marca_j`` must not react to the brand bought in the current
       visit: among first-time buyers, prior loyalty must be missing.
    """
    d = df.sort_values([cfg.KEY_CLIENT, cfg.KEY_TIME])
    first_visit = d.groupby(cfg.KEY_CLIENT).cumcount() == 0
    rows = []

    history = [c for c in FEATURE_FAMILIES["historial"] if c in d.columns]
    offenders = []
    for col in history:
        vals = d.loc[first_visit, col]
        if not bool(((vals == 0) | vals.isna()).all()):
            offenders.append(col)
    rows.append(
        {
            "verificacion": "Primera visita sin historial (features = 0 o NaN)",
            "features_evaluadas": len(history),
            "violaciones": len(offenders),
            "detalle": ", ".join(offenders) if offenders else "ninguna",
        }
    )

    expected = (
        d.groupby(cfg.KEY_CLIENT)[cfg.TARGET_INCIDENCE].cumsum() - d[cfg.TARGET_INCIDENCE]
    )
    mismatches = int((d["n_compras_previas"] != expected).sum()) if "n_compras_previas" in d else -1
    rows.append(
        {
            "verificacion": "n_compras_previas excluye la compra de la fila actual",
            "features_evaluadas": 1,
            "violaciones": mismatches,
            "detalle": "cumsum() - fila actual" if mismatches == 0 else "revisar construccion",
        }
    )

    loyalty = [f"lealtad_marca_{j}" for j in cfg.BRANDS if f"lealtad_marca_{j}" in d.columns]
    first_buy = first_visit & (d[cfg.TARGET_INCIDENCE] == 1)
    leak = 0
    if first_buy.any() and loyalty:
        leak = int((d.loc[first_buy, loyalty].fillna(0) > 0).sum().sum())
    rows.append(
        {
            "verificacion": "lealtad_marca no reacciona a la compra actual",
            "features_evaluadas": len(loyalty),
            "violaciones": leak,
            "detalle": f"{int(first_buy.sum())} primeras visitas con compra evaluadas",
        }
    )

    out = pd.DataFrame(rows)
    out["estado"] = out["violaciones"].map(lambda n: "OK" if n == 0 else "FALLA")
    out.attrs["sin_fuga"] = bool((out["violaciones"] == 0).all())
    return out


def verify_reference_fit(
    df: pd.DataFrame, reference: FeatureReference | None = None
) -> pd.DataFrame:
    """Control E10, feature side: prove the fitted statistics come only from train.

    Two assertions, and the second is the one that matters:

    1. Every statistic in ``reference`` equals the statistic recomputed on the
       train split alone.
    2. At least one of them **differs** from the same statistic computed over
       train+test. Without this check the first one would also pass on a
       reference that happens to be fitted on everything, and the control would
       be cosmetic.
    """
    reference = reference or df.attrs.get("reference") or _resolve_reference(df, None)
    train = df[df[cfg.KEY_TIME] <= cfg.SPLIT_DAY]
    on_train = fit_reference(train, n_bins=reference.n_bins)
    on_full = fit_reference(df, n_bins=reference.n_bins)

    rows = []
    differs_from_full = 0
    for group, getter in (
        ("precio_medio", lambda r: r.precio_medio),
        ("precio_max", lambda r: r.precio_max),
    ):
        for col in cfg.PRICE_COLS:
            got, exp, full = getter(reference)[col], getter(on_train)[col], getter(on_full)[col]
            differs_from_full += int(abs(exp - full) > 1e-12)
            rows.append(
                {
                    "estadistico": f"{group}[{col}]",
                    "ajustado": round(got, 6),
                    "solo_train": round(exp, 6),
                    "train+test": round(full, 6),
                    "coincide_con_train": bool(abs(got - exp) < 1e-9),
                }
            )
    for group, getter in (
        ("edad_bins", lambda r: r.edad_bins),
        ("ingreso_bins", lambda r: r.ingreso_bins),
    ):
        got, exp, full = getter(reference), getter(on_train), getter(on_full)
        differs_from_full += int(exp != full)
        rows.append(
            {
                "estadistico": group,
                "ajustado": str([round(e, 1) for e in got]),
                "solo_train": str([round(e, 1) for e in exp]),
                "train+test": str([round(e, 1) for e in full]),
                "coincide_con_train": bool(got == exp),
            }
        )

    out = pd.DataFrame(rows)
    out["estado"] = out["coincide_con_train"].map({True: "OK", False: "FALLA"})
    all_match = bool(out["coincide_con_train"].all())
    out.attrs["ajustado_solo_en_train"] = all_match
    out.attrs["n_estadisticos_que_difieren_del_frame_completo"] = differs_from_full
    out.attrs["distincion_material"] = differs_from_full > 0
    out.attrs["e10_features_ok"] = all_match and differs_from_full > 0
    out.attrs["lectura"] = (
        f"{len(out)} estadisticos ajustados solo en train (dias 1-{reference.dia_max_ajuste}, "
        f"{reference.n_filas_ajuste:,} filas); {differs_from_full} difieren del valor que "
        "tendrian ajustados sobre train+test, asi que la distincion no es cosmetica."
    )
    return out


def feature_summary(df: pd.DataFrame) -> pd.DataFrame:
    """One row per engineered feature: family, coverage and range.

    ``pct_nulos`` is expected to be non-zero for history features on a client's
    first visits -- that is absence of history, not a defect.
    """
    rows = []
    for family, names in FEATURE_FAMILIES.items():
        for name in names:
            if name not in df.columns:
                continue
            serie = df[name]
            rows.append(
                {
                    "familia": family,
                    "feature": name,
                    "pct_nulos": round(100 * float(serie.isna().mean()), 1),
                    "media": round(float(serie.mean()), 3),
                    "min": round(float(serie.min()), 3),
                    "max": round(float(serie.max()), 3),
                }
            )
    return pd.DataFrame(rows)

------------------------------------------------------------------------------
ARCHIVO: src/ferreyros/preprocess.py  (481 lineas)
------------------------------------------------------------------------------

"""Encoding and scaling inside a ``Pipeline`` fitted on train only.

Phase 2 of ``docs/brief-metodologia.md`` ends here: *"Codificación y escalado
dentro de un `Pipeline` de scikit-learn, ajustado **solo** sobre train."* This
module is also the one that closes control **E10** of
``docs/brief-alineamiento-eda.md``, the only blocking control the EDA notebook
left pending, because E10 cannot be verified before the pipeline exists.

Two leaks are possible at this stage and they are different:

* **Fitted feature statistics** -- the price mean a brand is compared against,
  the quantile edges of the demographic bins. Those live in
  :class:`ferreyros.features.FeatureReference`.
* **Fitted preprocessing statistics** -- the scaler's mean and variance, the
  encoder's category list. Those live here.

Both must come from train and nothing else, and :func:`verify_train_only_fit`
proves it instead of declaring it.

Missing values are *not* imputed with the mean. Per control E7, a client's
first visits have no history, and that absence is signal: an 8.5% block of
``NaN`` in the loyalty and recency features means "no purchase yet", not "value
unknown". The imputer fills a placeholder and ``add_indicator=True`` keeps the
absence itself as an explicit feature, so the model can learn from it.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

from dataclasses import dataclass

import numpy as np
import pandas as pd
from sklearn.compose import ColumnTransformer
from sklearn.impute import SimpleImputer
from sklearn.pipeline import Pipeline
from sklearn.preprocessing import OneHotEncoder, StandardScaler

from . import config as cfg
from . import data as data_mod
from . import features as feat_mod

# --------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Column roles -- declared, not guessed from dtype
# --------------------------------------------------------------------------

#: Unordered categories. One-hot encoded; ``ultima_marca_comprada`` includes
#: the ``0`` level, which is absence of history and a category of its own (E7).
#: ``segmento`` is the label of model M0 (Fase 3). It only appears when the
#: caller attached it with ``segmentation.attach_segment``; declaring it here is
#: what makes that attachment the single step needed to use it as a feature.
NOMINAL_COLS: list[str] = ["ocupacion", "ultima_marca_comprada", "dia_semana", "segmento"]

#: Ordered categories. Kept as a single number -- the order carries meaning,
#: so one-hot encoding would throw information away.
ORDINAL_COLS: list[str] = ["nivel_educacion", "edad_bin", "ingreso_bin"]

#: Already 0/1. Scaling them would only make the coefficients harder to read.
BINARY_COLS: list[str] = [
    "genero",
    "estado_civil",
    "ultima_cantidad_comprada",
    "promo_en_ultima_marca",
    *cfg.PROMO_COLS,
]

#: History features that are legitimately missing on a client's first visits.
#: They get the placeholder-plus-indicator treatment described in the module
#: docstring, never mean imputation.
HISTORY_GAP_COLS: list[str] = [
    "tasa_compra_historica",
    "dias_desde_ultima_compra",
    "cantidad_media_historica",
    "ticket_medio_historico",
    "n_marcas_distintas_previas",
    *[f"lealtad_marca_{j}" for j in cfg.BRANDS],
]

#: Placeholder for an absent history. The indicator column is what the model
#: reads; this value only has to be a constant the indicator makes identifiable.
HISTORY_FILL_VALUE = 0.0


def classify_columns(X: pd.DataFrame) -> dict[str, list[str]]:
    """Split the feature matrix into the four preprocessing roles.

    Anything not explicitly declared above and not a history gap is treated as
    a complete numeric feature. Returning the classification -- rather than
    hiding it inside the transformer -- lets the notebook show it as a table.
    """
    present = [c for c in X.columns]
    nominal = [c for c in NOMINAL_COLS if c in present]
    ordinal = [c for c in ORDINAL_COLS if c in present]
    binary = [c for c in BINARY_COLS if c in present]
    history = [c for c in HISTORY_GAP_COLS if c in present]
    assigned = {*nominal, *ordinal, *binary, *history}
    numeric = [c for c in present if c not in assigned]
    return {
        "nominal": nominal,
        "ordinal": ordinal,
        "binaria": binary,
        "historial_con_vacios": history,
        "numerica": numeric,
    }


def column_roles(X: pd.DataFrame) -> pd.DataFrame:
    """The classification as a table: role, treatment and reason."""
    groups = classify_columns(X)
    treatment = {
        "nominal": ("OneHotEncoder(handle_unknown='ignore')", "categoria sin orden"),
        "ordinal": ("mediana + StandardScaler", "el orden es informacion; se conserva como numero"),
        "binaria": ("passthrough", "ya es 0/1; escalarla solo complica la lectura"),
        "historial_con_vacios": (
            f"constante {HISTORY_FILL_VALUE} + indicador + StandardScaler",
            "el vacio es ausencia de historial (E7), no un valor desconocido",
        ),
        "numerica": ("mediana + StandardScaler", "continua y completa"),
    }
    rows = []
    for role, cols in groups.items():
        how, why = treatment[role]
        rows.append(
            {
                "rol": role,
                "n_columnas": len(cols),
                "tratamiento": how,
                "por_que": why,
                "columnas": ", ".join(cols[:4]) + (" ..." if len(cols) > 4 else ""),
            }
        )
    return pd.DataFrame(rows)


# --------------------------------------------------------------------------
# The pipeline
# --------------------------------------------------------------------------


def build_preprocessor(X: pd.DataFrame) -> ColumnTransformer:
    """Assemble the unfitted ``ColumnTransformer`` for the feature matrix.

    Unfitted on purpose: the object is built from the *column names* of ``X``
    and learns no statistic until :meth:`fit` is called on train.
    """
    groups = classify_columns(X)

    numeric_pipe = Pipeline(
        [("imputar", SimpleImputer(strategy="median")), ("escalar", StandardScaler())]
    )
    # The indicator is the point: it carries "this client had no history yet"
    # as a feature of its own instead of letting a filled value pretend to be
    # an observation.
    history_pipe = Pipeline(
        [
            (
                "imputar",
                SimpleImputer(
                    strategy="constant",
                    fill_value=HISTORY_FILL_VALUE,
                    add_indicator=True,
                ),
            ),
            ("escalar", StandardScaler()),
        ]
    )
    nominal_pipe = Pipeline(
        [
            ("imputar", SimpleImputer(strategy="most_frequent")),
            ("codificar", OneHotEncoder(handle_unknown="ignore", sparse_output=False)),
        ]
    )

    return ColumnTransformer(
        [
            ("numerica", numeric_pipe, groups["numerica"]),
            ("ordinal", numeric_pipe, groups["ordinal"]),
            ("historial", history_pipe, groups["historial_con_vacios"]),
            ("nominal", nominal_pipe, groups["nominal"]),
            ("binaria", "passthrough", groups["binaria"]),
        ],
        remainder="drop",
        verbose_feature_names_out=False,
    )


@dataclass
class PreparedData:
    """Everything the modelling phase needs, with the fit boundary explicit.

    ``preprocessor`` and ``reference`` are the two fitted objects of the
    project, and both were fitted on ``X_train`` only.
    """

    X_train: pd.DataFrame
    X_test: pd.DataFrame
    y_train: pd.Series
    y_test: pd.Series
    preprocessor: ColumnTransformer
    reference: feat_mod.FeatureReference
    feature_names: list[str]
    split_day: int
    groups_train: pd.Series
    groups_test: pd.Series

    @property
    def shapes(self) -> pd.DataFrame:
        """Row and column counts before and after preprocessing."""
        return pd.DataFrame(
            [
                {
                    "conjunto": "train",
                    "filas": len(self.X_train),
                    "cols_entrada": self.X_train.shape[1],
                    "cols_salida": len(self.feature_names),
                    "tasa_compra": round(float(self.y_train.mean()), 4),
                    "clientes": int(self.groups_train.nunique()),
                },
                {
                    "conjunto": "test",
                    "filas": len(self.X_test),
                    "cols_entrada": self.X_test.shape[1],
                    "cols_salida": len(self.feature_names),
                    "tasa_compra": round(float(self.y_test.mean()), 4),
                    "clientes": int(self.groups_test.nunique()),
                },
            ]
        )

    def transform(self, X: pd.DataFrame) -> pd.DataFrame:
        """Apply the train-fitted preprocessor, keeping the column names."""
        arr = self.preprocessor.transform(X)
        return pd.DataFrame(arr, columns=self.feature_names, index=X.index)


def prepare(
    df: pd.DataFrame | None = None,
    split_day: int | None = None,
    extra: list[str] | None = None,
) -> PreparedData:
    """Build the modelling matrices end to end, fitting only on train.

    The order is the whole point of this function:

    1. temporal split (control E8) -- decided *before* anything is fitted;
    2. :func:`ferreyros.features.fit_reference` on train only;
    3. features built over the full timeline so client history can cross the
       boundary backwards (control E6), using that train-fitted reference;
    4. the preprocessor fitted on the train rows only (control E10).

    Passing ``df=None`` loads the raw data, so the notebook can call
    ``prepare()`` and get a reproducible matrix in one line.

    ``extra`` names admissible columns already present in ``df`` that are not
    part of the standard feature set -- today only ``segmento``, the label of
    model M0. It is explicit on purpose: ``feature_matrix`` is a whitelist, so
    a column nobody declared can never reach the model by accident.
    """
    split_day = split_day or cfg.SPLIT_DAY
    if df is None:
        df = data_mod.add_paid_price_and_spend(data_mod.load_raw())

    train_raw, _ = data_mod.temporal_split(df, split_day)
    reference = feat_mod.fit_reference(train_raw)

    featured = feat_mod.build_features(df, reference=reference)
    train, test = data_mod.temporal_split(featured, split_day)

    X_train = feat_mod.feature_matrix(train, extra=extra)
    # Same columns in the same order on both sides, so the fitted transformer
    # can be applied to test without silent realignment.
    X_test = feat_mod.feature_matrix(test, extra=extra)[list(X_train.columns)]
    y_train = train[cfg.TARGET_INCIDENCE]
    y_test = test[cfg.TARGET_INCIDENCE]

    preprocessor = build_preprocessor(X_train).fit(X_train)
    names = [str(n) for n in preprocessor.get_feature_names_out()]

    return PreparedData(
        X_train=X_train,
        X_test=X_test,
        y_train=y_train,
        y_test=y_test,
        preprocessor=preprocessor,
        reference=reference,
        feature_names=names,
        split_day=split_day,
        groups_train=train[cfg.KEY_CLIENT],
        groups_test=test[cfg.KEY_CLIENT],
    )


# --------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Control E10 -- proven, not declared
# --------------------------------------------------------------------------


def verify_train_only_fit(prepared: PreparedData) -> pd.DataFrame:
    """Control E10: prove the preprocessor learned nothing from test.

    Four assertions. The second is the one that gives the control teeth: the
    first would also pass on a pipeline fitted over everything, so without a
    materiality check the control would be cosmetic.
